As someone who took pride in his precision and mentorship role, he experienced this shift as a profound threat to his identity and future, and over the following year he became increasingly withdrawn at work, missing safety meetings and turning down chances to train others on the new equipment. Around two years and three months ago, coworkers and his supervisor began noticing that he was having more difficulty concentrating on basic tasks and occasionally stepping away from his forklift mid‑shift, while at home his wife saw him abandon woodworking projects and skip church choir rehearsals. The combination of declining performance reviews, a written warning for safety concerns, and his wife’s alarm at how different he seemed from his usual steady, competitive self led them to contact their primary care physician, who, after ruling out obvious medical causes, referred him to psychiatry when it became clear his functioning had markedly deteriorated.\n- Chief Complaint: “I just don’t care about the things I used to love doing anymore, even my projects in the garage and going out on the river.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep thinking the company put some kind of tracking or listening device in the new forklifts and even in my work badge, like they’re watching me specifically and waiting for me to screw up so they can get rid of me, no matter how many times my wife says that’s crazy. \n- S020 (Hallucinations): At work and sometimes even at home in the garage, I hear this man’s voice right behind me saying things like, “You’re dangerous, you’re going to kill someone with that machine,” even when there’s nobody there and no radios on. \n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): My wife and coworkers say I’ll just stop the forklift in the middle of the aisle or stand in the kitchen at home staring at the counter for ten, fifteen minutes, kind of frozen, and I don’t even realize how long I’ve been just standing there until someone snaps me out of it. \n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Most days I feel like I’m moving and talking through mud, so I barely say two words at the dinner table, can’t show much on my face even when my grandkids are over, and I just sit in my chair instead of helping or joking around like I used to. \n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): The weirdest thing is I’ll go out to the garage, look at my half-built fishing boat or the guitars I used to fix up, and I just feel nothing—no excitement, no urge to pick up a tool—so I turn off the light and go back inside. \n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to pack away big plates after a shift, but now most nights my wife has to remind me to eat because I’m just not hungry and end up pushing the food around, dropping weight without even trying. \n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): I lie in bed staring at the ceiling for hours, then wake up two or three times a night with my mind racing or hearing that voice, and even on mornings I technically “slept,” I feel like I haven’t really rested at all. \n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Some days at the warehouse or even just walking from the living room to the bathroom, my legs feel like they’re made of concrete and I move so slow that people have to wait on me, and it takes me forever to get a sentence out when someone asks a simple question. \n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I can’t shake this feeling that I ruined everything—that the layoffs, the safety problems at work, even my wife having to pick up extra shifts are somehow my fault—and I catch myself thinking I don’t deserve to be around my family because all I do is drag them down.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: Around the time I stopped caring about my projects and started dragging myself through the day, that same stretch was when the voice really picked up and I became convinced the company was tracking me, so the hopelessness and the strange things in my head have basically been happening side by side.\n- depressive_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness_(Criterion_C): Even before the voice got so loud and the tracking idea took over, I’d already been feeling worn down, joyless, and guilty most days, and that heavy, depressed feeling has never really let up, even during times when the psychotic stuff has eased a bit.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode_(Criterion_B): There was a stretch last year when my wife thought I was doing a little better mood‑wise and I could get through the day without crying or feeling quite so crushed, but I was still sure my badge was bugged and kept hearing that man’s voice telling me I was dangerous even though I didn’t feel as deeply low right then.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve gone from being one of the go‑to guys at the warehouse to barely scraping by—getting written up for safety issues, turning down extra hours, skipping church and family get‑togethers, and needing my wife to stay on me about basic things like eating and paying the bills on time.\n- duration: Thinking back, this all really started to snowball not long after the big shake‑up at the warehouse, so it’s been going on for a little over two years now.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The belief that they’re tracking me and the man’s voice criticizing me have been a steady problem for well over a year and a half at this point.\n- depressive_episode_duration: I’ve felt this constant lack of interest, guilt, and heaviness most days since shortly after the restructuring began at work, so it’s been a little over two years The combination of declining performance reviews, a written warning for safety concerns, and his wife’s alarm at how different he seemed from his usual steady, competitive self led them to contact their primary care physician, who, after ruling out obvious medical causes, referred him to psychiatry when it became clear his functioning had markedly deteriorated.\n- Chief Complaint: “I just don’t when my grandkids are over, and I just sit in my chair instead of helping or joking around like I used to. \n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): The weirdest thing is I’ll go out to the garage, look at my half-built fishing boat or the guitars I used to fix up, and I just feel nothing—no excitement, no urge to pick up a tool—so I turn off the light and go back inside. \n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): made of concrete and I move so slow that people have to wait on me, and it takes me forever to get a sentence out when someone asks a simple question. \n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I can’t shake this feeling that I ruined everything—that the layoffs, the safety problems at work, even my wife having to pick up extra shifts are somehow my fault—and I catch myself thinking I don’t deserve to be around my family because all I do is drag them down.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_depressive_episode_occur_concurrently: Around the time I stopped caring about my Even before the voice got so loud and the tracking idea took over, I’d already been feeling worn down, joyless, and guilty most days, and that heavy, depressed feeling has never really let up, even man’s voice telling me I was dangerous even though I didn’t feel as deeply low right then.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, I’ve gone from being one of the go‑to guys at the warehouse to It feels like I don't even deserve to be around them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you would be better off dead or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I sometimes catch myself thinking they'd be better off without me because I just drag everyone down and don't deserve to be around them. But I don't have any actual plans to hurt myself or do anything like that. It's just this constant, heavy feeling that I'm a burden to my family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that other people could not, or felt that others were trying to harm you or that you were guilty of terrible things that were not true?"

Even during those couple of weeks, I still heard that man's voice telling me I was dangerous and remained convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had at least 2 weeks when the hallucinations or paranoid beliefs were still present even though you were not depressed or losing interest at all?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, that's right. There was a stretch last year for a few weeks where I wasn't feeling quite as deeply low or crying as much, and my mood seemed a bit better. But even during that time, I still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and I remained convinced my work badge was bugged.

I also feel completely hopeless and guilty, like I've ruined everything at work and am just dragging my family down. It feels like I don't even deserve to be around them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you would be better off dead or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I sometimes catch myself thinking they'd be better off without me because I just drag everyone down and don't deserve to be around them. But I don't have any actual plans to hurt myself or do anything like that. It's just this constant, heavy feeling that I'm a burden to my family.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard or seen things that other people could not, or felt that others were trying to harm you or that you were guilty of terrible things that were not true?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually... I keep hearing a man's voice right behind me at the warehouse telling me I'm dangerous and going to kill someone, even when there's nobody there. I'm also convinced the company is tracking me through my work badge and the new forklifts just to find an excuse to fire me, and I can't shake the guilt that I've somehow ruined everything for my family and caused the layoffs at work.\n\nDoctor: Did the hallucinations or paranoid beliefs ever happen for at least 2 weeks when you were not feeling depressed or losing interest, or do they only occur during your depressed periods?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a stretch last year for a few weeks where my mood felt a little better and I wasn't feeling quite so deeply low or crushed. But even during that time, I was still hearing that man's voice telling me I was dangerous and I was still sure they were tracking my work badge.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 weeks when the hallucinations or paranoid beliefs were still present even though you were not depressed or losing interest at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a stretch last year where my mood improved a bit and I wasn't crying as much or feeling quite as crushed. Even during those couple of weeks, I still heard that man's voice telling me I was dangerous and remained convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had at least 2 weeks when the hallucinations or paranoid beliefs were still present even though you were not depressed or losing interest at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. There was a stretch last year for a few weeks where I wasn't feeling quite as deeply low or crying as much, and my mood seemed a bit better. But even during that time, I still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and I remained convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week when you felt unusually high, irritable, or full of energy, needed much less sleep, or became more talkative or impulsive than usual?"

During that stretch last year when my mood was a bit better and I wasn't feeling as crushed, I still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and was sure my badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 weeks when you had hallucinations or strong false beliefs but did not have a major depressive episode at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly what happened during that stretch last year when my mood felt a bit better and I wasn't feeling quite so crushed. Even though the heavy depression wasn't as bad for those few weeks, I still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and remained convinced my work badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the voice or feeling paranoid, but you did not have a major depressive episode at the same time?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. There was a period last year for a few weeks when my mood lifted and I wasn't crying or feeling so deeply depressed. But even with my mood being better, I still heard that man's voice and was convinced my badge was bugged.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 weeks when you had hallucinations or strong false beliefs but did not have a major depressive episode at the same time?"
  }

Yes, there was a stretch last year when my mood lifted slightly and I wasn't feeling quite as deeply low or crying as much. But even during those few weeks, I still heard that man's voice calling me dangerous and remained convinced my work badge was bugged.

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings). Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future.  Subtypes: Future Hopelessness, Belief in Impossibility of Improvement, Helpless Resignation, Chronic Pessimism\n[S033] Elevated_Irritable_Mood: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.  Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).  Subtypes: Inflated Self-Confidence, Overestimation of Abilities, Grandiose Delusions\n[S036] Decreased_Sleep: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).  Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.
